# STATE — Durable System Checkpoint

**What this file is:** PURPOSE: aesop exists to let one orchestrator run autonomous development across many repos where an iteration counts only when it leaves receipts on disk that a gate can check (the receipts loop); status: real. The live durable checkpoint that Aesop itself uses during its own `/buildsystem` loop. It records the current system version, architectural decisions, known limitations, and the next milestone. This is not historical archive; it is read by the orchestrator to understand operational state.

## INTENT (pinned; the orchestrator reads this first after every compaction)
- Why pinned: Matt 2026-10-19, verbatim: "the workers context are small scope, your context is intent... over and over we are losing intent for context". The orchestrator holds intent; lanes hold files.
- Goal: aesop lets one orchestrator run autonomous development across many repos where an iteration counts only when it leaves receipts on disk that a gate can check (the receipts loop). Keyrun is its live demo, built end to end by Aesop (keyrun STATE.md INTENT governs keyrun work).
- Standing bar (Matt 2026-10-18): decide and report inside stated bounds; hard stops are deploys, spending, deletes, external messages. Hooks are friends: optimize, never delete, never fail open. Wrong data shown to a user is P0.
- How work moves: lanes navigate from the root CLAUDE.md "## Domain map", own named files, push and open PRs; the orchestrator verifies MERGED from gh state, never from narration, and checkpoints STATE.md/BUILDLOG.md with WHY at every safe boundary.
- MISSION (G0; Matt 2026-10-22 ~18:0xZ, verbatim): "your ultimate goal was to allow haikus to work as an ant colony to make discoveries, because people thought it was impossible" and "You are not considering nuance in niche domains which is why you were built, the machinery and everything is to help". Every goal below serves G0. A lane that returns a mechanical label where the domain has nuance has failed G0, not just its ticket: the fix is one scoped haiku per claim reading the evidence the product already holds (R-DYNASTY-3, event seq 240-241).
- GOAL TREE (stable ids; rulings and findings cite the id they serve):
  - G1 aesop: the receipts loop above (this file).
    - G1.1 per-project event stores (Matt 2026-10-22 ~17:5xZ, verbatim: "the play testers need to follow the same paradigm, actions left on disk in the event store, it can be a seperate event store but i just realized that is the whole issue. We have one event store... we need to decouple them and manage per project.. aesops job but that is causing extrme confusion and long reconcilliations, this also enables the multi orchestartion pattern"): aesop owns the event-store tooling; every project (aesop, keyrun, psinasty, the brain) gets its OWN store, rulings and checkpoints land in the store of the project they serve, the compaction hook reads each project's store by goal, and one orchestrator per project can run on its own store (multi-orchestration). Keyrun playtest bots leave their actions as events in keyrun's store (G2.4 receipts). The single shared conductor3 events/log store is the defect being fixed; migration keeps the hash chain verifiable.
  - G2 keyrun: the playable section-1 loop Matt can watch end to end (keyrun STATE.md INTENT). PIVOT (Matt 2026-10-22 ~17:5xZ, verbatim): "root cause why they spam one ability, the initial abilities will be like diablo... re pivot design.... abilities will have improvement trees, unlocked by progression, and gear will be dropped by completion. There is no guaranteed improvement at the end of every dungeon, its still RNG, its just that everyone gets a chance to roll instead of wow loot cap and trading garbage lol"
    - G2.1 ability spam: find the ROOT CAUSE of bots spamming one ability in the rotation code before any further tuning (f-47b1f102); a tuning PR that does not name the cause does not close it.
    - G2.2 ability improvement trees (Diablo-style): each initial ability grows through its own improvement tree, unlocked by progression; this is the ability-growth path, and BIBLE s7 "Facets change function" is amended to fit it.
    - G2.4 playtester guide (Matt 2026-10-22 ~17:5xZ, verbatim: "maybe the playtesters need a guide, not completely blind learning because they develop methods to cheese and not optimally play, which is a problem for testing lmao" and "the blind testers are more useful in how long it will take to learn"): two bot modes. GUIDED bots follow a per-class guide (intended rotation, kick ledger, mechanics) and are the balance/testing playtesters; BLIND bots keep learning from nothing and are measured only on time-to-learn, never used to judge balance. Ability spam and cheese from blind bots is a learning-time datum, not a balance signal.
    - G2.3 gear drops by completion, RNG: completing a dungeon gives every player a roll; no guaranteed improvement; supersedes BIBLE s7 "End of every run: choose one of three" (no WoW-style loot cap or trading).
  - G3 psinasty + dynasty: nuanced fantasy verdicts for Matt's league built ON the psinasty product's cache and engine facts (Matt 2026-10-22, verbatim: "use psinastys cache and setup for info, because all the infra is there"; "you need to consider other teams failure modes as well"); nothing unrefuted is shown; the strength rank and the title odds say what each measures. Artifacts: Dynasty Strategy GDxudaZFZKn9Z6dNA7LuzN, Power Rankings L5VjYJ7hM7YemeygErKuXS (stale at week 4).
- MEMORY TIERS (Matt 2026-10-22 ~18:3xZ, verbatim: "we built it together, you should know based on the architecture... but you raach for .md memories that need mechanical implementations, and eventually we forget long term tasks because we're overwriting the objective on every compact"): anything that must survive every compaction lives in THIS block, which the PreCompact hook reprints verbatim; memory .md files are recall-by-relevance and never the only home of a standing objective; the event store holds rulings that cite a G-id; the compaction summary's "what the next action serves" is derived from this tree, never from the recent window alone. When Matt states a goal, the orchestrator writes it here in the same turn, before any lane is dispatched.

## ACTIVE: RELEASE 0.10.0 + SELF-CLEAR (rotated 2026-10-15 16:50Z; read THIS block first; full historical ledger in BUILDLOG.md)
- 2026-10-22 DECISIONS (v0.10.4 checkpoint; each entry carries Matt's verbatim words and the WHY).
  - R-EVENTS-1 (state moves into an event store). Matt: "thats a byzantine fault", "state lives in the event store", "it should be pushed to git". WHY: the orchestrator is the untrusted node, so its context cannot be the state; state is append-only hash-chained events in conductor3 events/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl on branch events/log, projections come from tools/event_project.py, PreCompact injects projections (claude-config #247), and hooks validate against events. The disk record is the one thing every reader can check.
  - Verdict-ledger rule. Matt: "always ask why something broke". Every Matt verdict becomes an open_findings row with an observable, and the row is dispatched against before any work starts. A row closes only on proof of the observable, never on a PR (claude-scripts #142, #143; claude-config #246). WHY: a merged PR shows that code landed, not that the verdict is met.
  - Dispatch briefs are assembled from disk (claude-config #248). WHY: a brief written from context dies at compaction, so the brief has to be rebuilt from the same durable records every time.
  - npm miss, root cause: the verdict was closed on the GitHub PR instead of the npm page, and the follow-up lived only in context and died at compaction. Fix: v0.10.4 published and verified on npm. WHY: the observable for a publish verdict is the registry page, and follow-ups must be checkpointed to disk the moment they are known.

## STANDING RULES LEARNED

1. Run ListAgents before any dispatch after a /clear; previous-session lanes survive the clear and get double-dispatched.
2. A lane that fails is killed and relaunched with one complete brief; lane-pile-on denies SendMessage under 60s after a transcript write, so relaunch.
3. PR bodies: `gh pr create --body-file` with a unique filename, never a heredoc; the PR body ends with the footer "🐢 Generated with Aesop" only.
4. Scan committed work with `python ~/scripts/secret_scan.py --range origin/main..HEAD`; `--staged` refuses after a commit.
5. github-agent forbids `--auto`: brain-repo landings go to python-pro or general-purpose, or the brief says "--squash without --auto".
6. Never git stash; the primary tree is precious; worktrees are siblings only; use `git diff > patch.diff` then checkout and apply.
7. The orchestrator never runs merge_train.py or merge-queue polling; it dispatches, then verifies `state == MERGED` with gh.
8. A hook denial on the main thread is stop-and-report, never a detour (only the documented escape token with a logged reason).
9. Gate strengthening ships without asking; gate weakening never does.
10. Durable state over narration: every throughput or CI claim needs gh counts; a report from a ceiling-denied session is unverified until bound with gh.
11. Self-clear path: checkpoint first, then `auto_clear.py --check`, then clear_watcher in watch mode with the resume text; a watcher that cannot parse the post-clear screen must log and exit, not loop.
12. No-delete rule: classify worktrees and remove only clean and merged ones; never restart the user's browser.

It records the current system version, architectural decisions, known limitations, and the next milestone. This is not historical archive; it is read by the orchestrator to understand operational state.
